{"forms":[{"form":"hammers out","tags":["present","singular","third-person"]},{"form":"hammering out","tags":["participle","present"]},{"form":"hammered out","tags":["participle","past"]},{"form":"hammered out","tags":["past"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"verb","senses":[{"examples":[{"text":"A smith hammers out iron on an anvil.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To flatten and shape (metal) by hammering."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"I watched a tinsmith hammer out a funnel.","type":"example"},{"text":"The fender was so badly damaged that it couldn’t be hammered out.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To create (something) by hammering metal; to repair (something made of metal that has become bent or dented) by hammering."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"The bolt wouldn’t budge so she tried hammering it out.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To remove (something tightly in place) by hammering."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"The kids are forever hammering out “Heart and Soul” on the piano.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To produce (a sound or signal) by hammering."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"glosses":["To type (something) on a keyboard."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"It took me all night to hammer out the first draft of my essay.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To create or develop (an idea, plan, piece of writing, etc.) with effort."],"tags":["figuratively","transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"Two years after the flood, my lawyer managed to hammer out a settlement with my insurance company over the damages.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To come to (a difficult agreement or settlement) after much arguing and conflict."],"tags":["figuratively","transitive"]}],"upstream_line":817049,"upstream_offset":3085502966,"upstream_record_sha256":"0867bdbdb6182bb2ba9c0f79dea5aabada0a48f61e3f786630247f980058f917","word":"hammer out"}

{"forms":[{"form":"turns around","tags":["present","singular","third-person"]},{"form":"turning around","tags":["participle","present"]},{"form":"turned around","tags":["participle","past"]},{"form":"turned around","tags":["past"]}],"lang_code":"en","pos":"verb","senses":[{"examples":[{"text":"At the end of the road, we turned around and walked back to the hotel.","type":"example"},{"text":"The world turns around once every twenty-four hours.","type":"example"},{"text":"Turn around once or twice so I can see your new dress.","type":"example"},{"text":"A wheel turns around on an axle.","type":"example"},{"text":"If you kids don't cut it out I will turn this car around!","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To physically rotate (usually around a vertical axis) for a half turn (180 degrees), a whole turn (360 degrees), or an indefinite amount."],"synonyms":[{"word":"twirl"}],"tags":["ergative"]},{"examples":[{"text":"She turned her position around and now she is in favor of the merger.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To change drastically in a fundamental way, often for the better; to change to the opposite (opinion or position)."],"tags":["figuratively","transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"The new management team has really turned the company around 180 degrees, and they expect a good profit next year.","type":"example"},{"text":"The team really turned it around in the second half for a great win.","type":"example"},{"text":"They were way ahead but the game turned around on them and they lost 4–3.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To reverse an expected outcome (of a game, etc.), usually from a losing outcome to a winning one; to return (a business, department, etc.) to effectiveness, profitability, etc."],"tags":["transitive"],"topics":["business","hobbies","lifestyle","management","sports"]},{"glosses":["To convert (an agent) to work for one's own side."],"tags":["transitive"],"topics":["espionage","government","military","politics","war"]},{"examples":[{"text":"You can't just turn around and say that it was all my fault.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To suddenly change or reverse one's opinion, point of view, stated position, behaviour, etc."],"tags":["colloquial","idiomatic","intransitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"Let's turn that around and look at it from another angle.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To consider from a different viewpoint."],"raw_tags":["of an idea"],"tags":["idiomatic","transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"We can turn around 500 units by next week.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["(often with a unit of time) To produce; to output; to generate."],"tags":["colloquial","idiomatic","transitive"]}],"sounds":[],"upstream_line":558571,"upstream_offset":2548557280,"upstream_record_sha256":"a7dea0f0c8d84029ef6b4970a9df0dc08a724e9196d2b9f1fc1702accb85f61a","word":"turn around"}
